\begin{abstract}
This record states, for every object of 7+1.nb, where it comes from (the task statement, the notebook 4+4.nb, or the literature), the equation that defines it, how it was derived, and how it was checked: the full list of the 479 checks and their results, the independent Python cross-checks, and the hashes of the files. Sections 1–3 concern the first stage (sections 0–6 of the notebook: octonions and Clifford algebras), section 6 the second stage (sections 7–10: the Dirac equation and the Kohn–Sham theories of the field dirac16complex), section 7 the third stage (sections 11–19: gravity, the self-coupled field in gravitational fields, the Standard Model), section 8 the fourth stage (sections 20–23: the coupled field equations and their solutions in the three gravity regimes), section 9 the analysis of section 24 (dirac16complex as dark matter and dark energy), section 10 sections 25–26 (the auxiliary torus, the world brane and the radion).
\end{abstract}

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{1.1 The task statement}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{1.1 The task statement}
The task asked for a new Mathematica notebook 7+1.nb in \texttt{D:\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Developer\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}github\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}7+1} defining a new realization of the octonions and a new representation of the Clifford algebra of an 8-dimensional vector space that mirror, as far as possible, the split octonions and the pseudo-Clifford algebra of 4+4.nb; real numbers and the real 8×8 and 16×16 matrices of 4+4.nb were to be used if possible, otherwise their complexification; examples were to check all work; notation was to be changed and documented where confusing. It fixed the coordinates
\begin{align*}
\text{4+4.nb:}\quad &X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ x_0=\text{hidden space-like},\ x_1,x_2,x_3=\text{3-space}, \\
&\phantom{X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ }x_4=\text{time},\ x_5,x_6,x_7=\text{auxiliary time-like}, \\
\text{7+1.nb:}\quad &X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ x_0=\text{time (octonion unit)},\ x_1,x_2,x_3=\text{3-space}, \\
&\phantom{X=(x_0,\dots,x_7),\ }x_4,\dots,x_7=\text{auxiliary space-like},
\end{align*}
and gave two definitions. [1A] the octonions: $e_0=1$, $e_i^2=-1$, Fano plane, Cayley–Dickson doubling $\mathbb O=\mathbb H\oplus\mathbb H$, conjugation $\bar x=x_0-\sum_ix_ie_i$ with $\overline{xy}=\bar y\bar x$, $N(x)=x\bar x=\sum_Ax_A^2$, $N(xy)=N(x)N(y)$, $x^{-1}=\bar x/N(x)$, alternativity, power-associativity. [1B] the Clifford algebra $Cl(V,Q)=\mathcal T(V)/\mathcal I_Q(V)$ with $v^2=Q(v)1$, $vw+wv=2B(v,w)1$, $B(v,w)=\frac12(Q(v+w)-Q(v)-Q(w))$, dimension $2^8=256$, a classification table for $p+q=8$, and $Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)$. Section 4 lists the statements of [1A] and [1B] that had to be corrected.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{1.3 Literature}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{1.3 Literature}
\begin{itemize}
\item J. C. Baez, \emph{The Octonions}, Bull. Amer. Math. Soc. 39 (2002) 145–205, arXiv:math/0105155, section 2.2 (the page https://math.ucr.edu/home/baez/octonions/node5.html, which the task statement itself cites, was read on 2026-10-26, when 7+1.nb was first written): the Cayley–Dickson product $(a,b)(c,d)=(ac-db^{*},\ a^{*}d+cb)$, where Baez writes $x^{*}$ for the conjugate $\bar x$ and the conjugate of a pair is $(a,b)^{*}=(a^{*},-b)$. 7+1.nb proves that its octonions satisfy this product with $x=a+\ell b$, $\ell=e_4$.
\item H. B. Lawson and M.-L. Michelsohn, \emph{Spin Geometry}, Princeton (1989): classification of real Clifford algebras, $Cl(p+1,q+1)\cong Cl(p,q)\otimes\mathbb R(2)$. 7+1.nb does not rely on it: it computes the classification directly (section 2.10 below).
\item Standard facts used in proofs: Schur's lemma (the intertwiners of an irreducible module form a division algebra; over $\mathbb C$, the scalars); $Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)$ has a unique irreducible module $\mathbb C^{16}$; Hurwitz's theorem (a unital composition algebra of dimension 8 is the octonions if its norm is positive definite and the split octonions if the norm is indefinite); Degen's eight-square identity.
\end{itemize}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{1.4 The signature: the author’s explanation of 27 October 2026}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{1.4 The signature: the author’s explanation of 27 October 2026}
\textbf{Source.} On 27 October 2026 the author of the project explained that the xAct list $\{7,1,0\}$ declares seven space-like dimensions and one time-like dimension, while $\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis, and asked that this be made clear in all documentation, examples and provenance records. \textbf{What it corrected.} The version of 7+1.nb pushed in the commits 52d8729 to dc2f178 of github.com/once-ere/7plus1 declared the xAct metric of section 12 with \texttt{DefMetric[\{1, 7, 0\}, …]}, reading the list as the signs of $\eta^{(1,7)}$; since then section 12 declares \texttt{DefMetric[\{7, 1, 0\}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]}, exactly as the author’s notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb does. \textbf{What was added.} Checks in 7+1.nb — section 4.7: $\eta^{(1,7)}$ is the squares of the octonion basis, $e_Ae_A=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AA}e_0$ and $\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}$; section 12.2: \texttt{SignatureOfMetric[g71]} $=\{7,1,0\}$ with \texttt{SignDetOfMetric[g71]} $=-1$, $\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}$, and $\mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}x^Ax^B$; section 17: the flat metric $\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,-1,-1)$ of the world brane is the squares of the basis $e_0,e_1,e_2,e_3$ of the quaternion subalgebra — and the same statements in the Python programs \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref71.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}gravity.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}sm.py}; the explanation itself in the text of 7+1.nb (sections 1.2, 1.4, 4.2, 4.7, 7, 11, 12.2, 13, 15.1, 17) and in every document, symbol file and provenance file of the repository. \textbf{What did not change.} No result. Compared output by output with the backup below, every output of 7+1.nb is the same, except the declared list itself, the new checks and the check counts (and, as in every run, the name of the private context and the measured times); in particular every component of every Lovelock tensor is unchanged. The components follow from the component matrix of the metric, and the sign of the determinant, \texttt{SignDetOfMetric}, is $-1$ for both lists. \textbf{Backup.} The evaluated notebook as it was before is kept in the workspace as \texttt{backups/\allowbreak{}7+1\_\allowbreak{}2026-10-27\_\allowbreak{}before\_\allowbreak{}signature.nb} (the folder backups/ is excluded from the repository by .gitignore), 1034401 bytes, SHA-256 \texttt{06094248\allowbreak{}8caf4f45\allowbreak{}120ffd37\allowbreak{}be0123e9\allowbreak{}6992a31c\allowbreak{}d7fdbde0\allowbreak{}9ee4dc4c\allowbreak{}ed4f5d3a}; \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verify\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py} compares every input, text and section cell of 7+1.nb with it and finds every cell present, each one unchanged or changed by this explanation, and it compares every output in the same way.

\textbf{The explanation, in full.} The 7+1 spacetime has one time-like dimension ($x_0$, the direction of the octonion unit $e_0=1$) and seven space-like dimensions ($x_1,x_2,x_3$ ordinary space, $x_4,\dots,x_7$ auxiliary space). Two different objects describe this, and neither may be replaced by the other. (i) The tensor package xAct, used in section 12 of 7+1.nb exactly as in the author’s notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb, declares the metric with \texttt{DefMetric[\{7, 1, 0\}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]}: the list $\{7,1,0\}$ declares seven space-like dimensions, one time-like dimension and no degenerate dimension — the numbers of entries $+1$ and $-1$ of $\eta^{(7,1)}=\mathrm{diag}(-1,+1,\dots,+1)$ (\texttt{η71}), the same spacetime written in the sign convention of 4+4.nb (space-like $=+1$, time-like $=-1$). (ii) The matrix $\eta^{(1,7)}=-\eta^{(7,1)}=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$ (\texttt{η17}) is simply the squares of the octonion basis: $e_0^2=+1$ for the unit $e_0=1$ (time) and $e_i^2=-1$ for the seven imaginary units $e_1,\dots,e_7$ (space); more generally it is the real part of the product of two basis units, and it gives the quadratic form of the octonions:
\begin{gather*}
\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B),\qquad e_0^2=+1,\qquad e_i^2=-1\ \ (i=1,\dots,7),\\ \mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=x_0^2-x_1^2-\cdots-x_7^2=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}\,x^Ax^B\qquad (x=x^Ae_A\in\mathbb O).
\end{gather*}
$\eta^{(1,7)}$ gives the signs of the Clifford relation $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B+\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A=2\,\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}\,1_{16}$ of the real 16×16 Dirac matrices: time squares to $+1_{16}$ and each of the seven space directions to $-1_{16}$, exactly like the octonion units (in the notation $Cl(p,q)$, with $p$ generators squaring to $+1$ and $q$ to $-1$, the algebra $Cl(1,7)\cong\mathbb R(16)$). It also gives the signs of the metric components: $g_{00}>0$ along the time-like direction, negative squares along the space-like directions. Every component computed by 7+1.nb follows from the component matrix of the metric; the declared list fixes for xAct the sign of the determinant of the metric, \texttt{SignDetOfMetric[g71]} $=-1$, which is the same in both sign conventions, since $\det \eta^{(1,7)}=\det \eta^{(7,1)}=-1$. 7+1.nb checks \texttt{SignatureOfMetric[g71]} $=\{7,1,0\}$, \texttt{SignDetOfMetric[g71]} $=-1$, $\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}$ and $\mathrm{Re}(x\,x)=\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}x^Ax^B$.

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{2. Derivations: how each new object was obtained}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{2. Derivations: how each new object was obtained}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.1 Octonion matrices \texorpdfstring{$\tau^{O}$}{τO}}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.1 Octonion matrices \texorpdfstring{$\tau^{O}$}{τO}}
Origin: the split matrices of 4+4.nb and the observation (checked) that $\sigma=\tau_1\tau_2\tau_3=\tau_4\tau_5\tau_6\tau_7$ commutes with $\tau_1,\tau_2,\tau_3$, anticommutes with $\tau_4,\dots,\tau_7$ and squares to $1_8$. Hence
\begin{align*}
\tau^{O}_0&=1_8,\quad \tau^{O}_A=\tau_A\ (A\le3),\quad \tau^{O}_k=\tau_k\sigma\ (k\ge4),\qquad (\tau_k\sigma)^2=\tau_k\sigma\tau_k\sigma=-\tau_k^2\sigma^2=-1_8, \\
\tau^{O}_h\tau^{O}_k&=\tau_h\tau_k\sigma=-\tau_k\tau_h\sigma=-\tau_k\sigma\tau_h=-\tau^{O}_k\tau^{O}_h,\qquad \tau^{O}_k\tau^{O}_l=\tau_k\sigma\tau_l\sigma=-\tau_k\tau_l=\tau_l\tau_k=-\tau^{O}_l\tau^{O}_k\quad(k\neq l\ge4).
\end{align*}
This is the real counterpart of the complex substitution $\tau_k\to i\tau_k$ that turns split octonions into octonions over $\mathbb C$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.2 Octonion vielbein and multiplication constants}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.2 Octonion vielbein and multiplication constants}
Origin: In[294] and In[325] of 4+4.nb with the split spinor metric $\sigma$ replaced by the Euclidean $g^{O}=1_8$ and the same unit spinor $u$:
\begin{align*}
(E^{O})_{Ba}&=\big(u^{\mathsf T}g^{O}\bar\tau^{O}_B\big)_a,\qquad L^{O}_A=g^{O}_{AA}E^{O}\tau^{O}_A(E^{O})^{-1},\qquad e_Ae_K=\sum_L(L^{O}_A)_{LK}e_L .
\end{align*}
Why the result is the octonion algebra: $(E^{O})^{-1}e_0=u$ makes $e_0$ a two-sided unit, and $\tau^{O}(x)\bar\tau^{O}(x)=N(x)1_8$ together with the orthogonality of $E^{O}$ gives $N(xy)=N(x)N(y)$. A unital composition algebra of dimension 8 with a positive definite norm is the octonions (Hurwitz). The notebook checks all the laws directly.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.3 Cayley–Dickson form}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.3 Cayley–Dickson form}
Origin: Baez 2002, section 2.2. With $\ell=e_4$, $x=a+\ell b$, $a=(x_0,\dots,x_3)$, $b=(x_4,\dots,x_7)$:
\begin{equation*}
(a,b)(c,d)=\big(ac+\gamma\,d\bar b,\ \bar a d+cb\big),\qquad \gamma=-1\ (\text{7+1.nb}),\qquad \gamma=+1\ (\text{4+4.nb}).
\end{equation*}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.4 Euclidean mirror \texorpdfstring{$T^{O}$}{TO}}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.4 Euclidean mirror \texorpdfstring{$T^{O}$}{TO}}
Origin: In[98] of 4+4.nb with $\tau\to\tau^{O}$: $T^{O}_A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\bar\tau^{O}_A\\\tau^{O}_A&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, so that $\lbrace T^{O}_A,T^{O}_B\rbrace=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\bar\tau^{O}_A\tau^{O}_B+\bar\tau^{O}_B\tau^{O}_A&0\\0&\tau^{O}_A\bar\tau^{O}_B+\tau^{O}_B\bar\tau^{O}_A\end{smallmatrix}\right)=2\delta_{AB}1_{16}$. In terms of 4+4.nb: $T^{O}_A=T_A$ ($A\le3$), $T^{O}_k=T_kS_{44}$ ($k\ge4$), $S_{44}=T_4T_5T_6T_7=\mathrm{diag}(\sigma,\sigma)$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.5 Real 7+1 generators \texorpdfstring{$\Gamma^{(1,7)}$}{Γ(1,7)}}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.5 Real 7+1 generators \texorpdfstring{$\Gamma^{(1,7)}$}{Γ(1,7)}}
Origin: the standard construction of a Lorentzian Clifford algebra from a Euclidean one, applied to $T^{O}$:
\begin{align*}
\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0&=T^{O}_0,\qquad \Gamma^{(1,7)}_i=T^{O}_0T^{O}_i,\qquad (T^{O}_0T^{O}_i)^2=-(T^{O}_0)^2(T^{O}_i)^2=-1_{16}, \\
\lbrace T^{O}_0,T^{O}_0T^{O}_i\rbrace&=T^{O}_i+T^{O}_0T^{O}_iT^{O}_0=T^{O}_i-T^{O}_i=0,\qquad \lbrace T^{O}_0T^{O}_i,T^{O}_0T^{O}_j\rbrace=-\lbrace T^{O}_i,T^{O}_j\rbrace=-2\delta_{ij}1_{16}.
\end{align*}
Hence $\lbrace\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B\rbrace=2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}$ — the generators square like the octonion basis, since $\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)$ ($e_0^2=+1$, $e_i^2=-1$) — and $\Gamma^{(1,7)}(x)^2=\mathrm{Re}(xx)1_{16}$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.6 Complexified generators \texorpdfstring{$\Gamma^{(7,1)}$}{Γ(7,1)}}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.6 Complexified generators \texorpdfstring{$\Gamma^{(7,1)}$}{Γ(7,1)}}
Origin: the task's instruction to use the complexification of the 4+4 matrices if real numbers fail. Only the time block is multiplied by $i$: $\tau^{C}_0=\bar\tau^{C}_0=i1_8$, $\tau^{C}_k=\tau^{O}_k$; then $\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&\bar\tau^{C}_A\\\tau^{C}_A&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, $(\Gamma^{(7,1)}_0)^2=i^2\,1_{16}=-1_{16}$, and $\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=S(i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A)S^{-1}$ with $S=(1_{16}-i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0)/\sqrt2$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.7 The impossibility theorem}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.7 The impossibility theorem}
Origin: Schur's lemma and the uniqueness of the irreducible module of $Cl_8(\mathbb C)\cong\mathbb C(16)$. The intertwiners $M$ with $M\overline{\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A}=\Gamma^{(7,1)}_AM$ are computed in 7+1.nb with \texttt{NullSpace}; they form the line through $M_0=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-1_8\\1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, and $M_0\overline{M_0}=-1_{16}$. A real realization $R_A=T\Gamma^{(7,1)}_AT^{-1}$ would give an intertwiner $M=T^{-1}\overline T$ with $M\overline M=1_{16}$, impossible because $M=cM_0$ gives $M\overline M=-|c|^21_{16}$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.8 Real 32×32 generators}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.8 Real 32×32 generators}
Origin: realification. $\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A=\varepsilon\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A$ with $\varepsilon^2=-1_2$ gives $\lbrace\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A,\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_B\rbrace=\varepsilon^2\otimes2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}=2\eta^{(7,1)}_{AB}1_{32}$. Commutant: $Q_i=\varepsilon\otimes1_{16}$, $Q_j=\sigma_1\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$, $Q_k=\sigma_3\otimes\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$, found from $\varepsilon\sigma_1=-\sigma_1\varepsilon$, $\varepsilon\sigma_3=-\sigma_3\varepsilon$ and $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A=-\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.9 Examples}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.9 Examples}
so(1,7): $\Sigma_{AB}=\frac14[\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_B]$; the commutation relations follow from the Clifford relation. Boost and rotation: $(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0\Gamma^{(1,7)}_1)^2=1_{16}$, $(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_4\Gamma^{(1,7)}_5)^2=-1_{16}$, so $e^{\beta\Sigma_{01}}=\cosh\frac\beta2+\sinh\frac\beta2\Gamma^{(1,7)}_0\Gamma^{(1,7)}_1$ and $e^{\theta\Sigma_{45}}=\cos\frac\theta2+\sin\frac\theta2\Gamma^{(1,7)}_4\Gamma^{(1,7)}_5$. Dirac operator: $(\Gamma^A\partial_A)^2=\frac12\lbrace\Gamma^A,\Gamma^B\rbrace\partial_A\partial_B=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AB}\partial_A\partial_B$. Real amplitudes: $p=(3,2,2,0,0,0,0,0)$, $p^{\mathsf T}\eta^{(1,7)}p=1$, $\not p^{\,2}=1_{16}$; the real matrix $\not p$ has a real 8-dimensional eigenspace for the eigenvalue $+1$.

Real spinor fields. If $J$ anticommutes with every $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A$, then $J\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$ commutes with every $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A$ (because $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$ anticommutes with each of them), so by irreducibility $J\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=c\,1_{16}$ and $J=-c\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$ (as $(\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8)^{-1}=-\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$), whose square is $-c^2\,1_{16}$. Hence the real operator $\Gamma^A\partial_A-mJ$ has the square $\partial_0^2-\dots-\partial_7^2-c^2m^2$ (a tachyon), and a real equation $(G^A\partial_A-m)\Psi=0$ implies the Klein–Gordon equation only if the real $G_A$ obey the $\eta^{(7,1)}$ relation, impossible for 16×16 matrices (2.7). Massless: for $k=(1,1,0,\dots,0)$, $\not k^{\,2}=k^{\mathsf T}\eta^{(1,7)}k\,1_{16}=0$ and $\Gamma^A\partial_A[w\cos(k\cdot x)]=-\sin(k\cdot x)\,\not k\,w$, which vanishes for the 8 real solutions of $\not k\,w=0$. Massive, with the real 32×32 matrices: $P=\sum_Ap^A\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A$, $P^2=-m^2\,1_{32}$, and with $\phi=p\cdot x$, $\widehat\Gamma^A=\eta_{(7,1)}^{AA}\widehat\Gamma^{(7,1)}_A$:
\begin{align*}
\widehat\Gamma^{A}\partial_A\Big(w\cos\phi+\tfrac1mPw\sin\phi\Big)&=-P\Big(-w\sin\phi+\tfrac1mPw\cos\phi\Big) \\
&=Pw\sin\phi+m\,w\cos\phi=m\Big(w\cos\phi+\tfrac1mPw\sin\phi\Big),
\end{align*}
using $\partial_A\phi=(\eta^{(1,7)}p)_A$ and $\eta_{(7,1)}^{AA}\eta^{(1,7)}_{AA}=-1$.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{2.10 Classification of the nine signatures}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{2.10 Classification of the nine signatures}
For each $p$, complex generators $G_A=T^{O}_A$ ($A<p$), $G_A=iT^{O}_A$ ($A\ge p$) satisfy $\lbrace G_A,G_B\rbrace=\pm2\delta_{AB}1_{16}$ with $p$ plus signs. The indicator sign of $M\overline M$ ($M\overline{G_A}=G_AM$) is $+1$ for $p\in\lbrace8,5,4,1,0\rbrace$ ($\mathbb R(16)$) and $-1$ for $p\in\lbrace7,6,3,2\rbrace$ ($\mathbb H(8)$). An independent numerical method (\texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}classify\_\allowbreak{}clifford.py}: commutant of the realified 32-dimensional representation, whose traceless part has a negative definite trace form exactly in the quaternionic case) gives the same table. Explicit real generators: $T^{O}$ (8,0), $\Gamma^{(5,3)}_0=T_0$, $\Gamma^{(5,3)}_i=T_0T_i$ (5,3) with $\eta^{(5,3)}=\mathrm{diag}(1,-1,-1,-1,1,1,1,1)$, $T$ (4,4), $\Gamma^{(1,7)}$ (1,7), $\Gamma^{(0,8)}_A=T^{O}_8T^{O}_A$ (0,8).

439 & \texttt{explicit forms: every Gamma17[A] is a signed permutation matrix (one entry +-1 in each row and column), and 2 Sqrt[2] U has only the entries 0, +-1, +-I, eight non-zero ones in each row and in each column} & True \\
440 & \texttt{brane spinor (x) transverse spinor: Gamma17[mu].Gamma17[nu] == U.(gamma\_\allowbreak{}mu.gamma\_\allowbreak{}nu (x) 1\_\allowbreak{}4).U\textasciicircum{}dag, Gamma17[k].Gamma17[l] == U.(1\_\allowbreak{}4 (x) rho\_\allowbreak{}k.rho\_\allowbreak{}l).U\textasciicircum{}dag and Gamma17[mu].Gamma17[k] == U.(gamma\_\allowbreak{}mu.gamma5 (x) rho\_\allowbreak{}k).U\textasciicircum{}dag (mu, nu = 0..3, k, l = 4..7): the Lorentz group of the brane acts on the brane spinor index, the rotations about the brane on the transverse index, and the generators that mix them are broken by the brane} & True \\
441 & \texttt{the transverse index carries Spin(4) = SU(2) x SU(2): rho4567 == -gamma5 (4 x 4), it commutes with every rho\_\allowbreak{}k.rho\_\allowbreak{}l and anticommutes with every rho\_\allowbreak{}k; for each even permutation (k, l, m, n) of (4, 5, 6, 7) the combination rho\_\allowbreak{}k.rho\_\allowbreak{}l + s rho\_\allowbreak{}m.rho\_\allowbreak{}n vanishes on the eigenspace rho4567 = s, and rho\_\allowbreak{}k.rho\_\allowbreak{}l - s rho\_\allowbreak{}m.rho\_\allowbreak{}n acts there with rank 2: the four values of a form (2, 1) + (1, 2)} & True \\

470 & \texttt{RS1: M\_\allowbreak{}Pl\textasciicircum{}2 = (1/\allowbreak{}kappa) Int\_\allowbreak{}(-Pi R)\textasciicircum{}(Pi R) e\textasciicircum{}(-2 k |y|) dy = (1 - e\textasciicircum{}(-2 Pi k R))/\allowbreak{}(kappa k)} & True \\
471 & \texttt{RS1 radion: with R promoted to T(x), the bulk action is (3/\allowbreak{}(kappa k)) (d e\textasciicircum{}(-k Pi T))\textasciicircum{}2 + V(T) + (a total derivative), so the canonical radion is mu = Sqrt[6/\allowbreak{}(kappa k)] e\textasciicircum{}(-k Pi T) (the note: Sqrt[12/\allowbreak{}(kappa k)])} & True \\
472 & \texttt{eight dimensions, warped along one extra direction (ds\textasciicircum{}2 = e\textasciicircum{}(-2 A(y)) eta - dy\textasciicircum{}2 - dz.dz): G\textasciicircum{}mu\_\allowbreak{}nu = 3 A'' - 6 A'\textasciicircum{}2, G\textasciicircum{}y\_\allowbreak{}y = -6 A'\textasciicircum{}2, G\textasciicircum{}z\_\allowbreak{}z = 4 A'' - 10 A'\textasciicircum{}2; with only Lambda (all G\textasciicircum{}A\_\allowbreak{}A equal) A'' = 0 and A' = 0: the note's five-dimensional equations do not carry over} & True \\
473 & \texttt{Goldberger-Wise in RS1: the static bulk scalar (Sqrt|G| = e\textasciicircum{}(-4 k y), G\textasciicircum{}yy = -1) obeys Phi'' - 4 k Phi' - m\textasciicircum{}2 Phi = 0, solved by e\textasciicircum{}(2 k y)(C1 e\textasciicircum{}(nu k y) + C2 e\textasciicircum{}(-nu k y)), nu = Sqrt[4 + m\textasciicircum{}2/\allowbreak{}k\textasciicircum{}2] = 2 + eps + O(m\textasciicircum{}4), eps = m\textasciicircum{}2/\allowbreak{}(4 k\textasciicircum{}2)} & True \\
474 & \texttt{Goldberger-Wise radion mass: V(mu) = 4 k (mu/\allowbreak{}f)\textasciicircum{}4 (v\_\allowbreak{}B - v\_\allowbreak{}A (mu/\allowbreak{}f)\textasciicircum{}eps)\textasciicircum{}2 has V'(<mu>) = 0 at (<mu>/\allowbreak{}f)\textasciicircum{}eps = v\_\allowbreak{}B/\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}A (k Pi <T> = (4 k\textasciicircum{}2/\allowbreak{}m\textasciicircum{}2) Log[v\_\allowbreak{}A/\allowbreak{}v\_\allowbreak{}B]) and V''(<mu>) = 8 k eps\textasciicircum{}2 v\_\allowbreak{}B\textasciicircum{}2 <mu>\textasciicircum{}2/\allowbreak{}f\textasciicircum{}4; with f\textasciicircum{}2 = 6/\allowbreak{}(kappa k): m\_\allowbreak{}r\textasciicircum{}2 = (4/\allowbreak{}3) kappa k\textasciicircum{}2 eps\textasciicircum{}2 v\_\allowbreak{}B\textasciicircum{}2 (<mu>/\allowbreak{}f)\textasciicircum{}2 (the note's f\textasciicircum{}2 = 12/\allowbreak{}(kappa k) gives 2/\allowbreak{}3)} & True \\*
\end{longtable}}
\Needspace{6\baselineskip}\subsubsection*{26.6 The Higgs boson on the brane and the radion: mixing (the note’s formulas, checked), and its size in 7+1}
{\footnotesize
\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr 0.0700\linewidth-2\tabcolsep\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr 0.8100\linewidth-2\tabcolsep\relax}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{\dimexpr 0.1200\linewidth-2\tabcolsep\relax}}

475 & \texttt{conformally flat brane metric h = Omega\textasciicircum{}2 eta (four dimensions, Omega(x0, x1)): Sqrt|h| R(h) == -6 Omega box Omega with box = d0\textasciicircum{}2 - d1\textasciicircum{}2 (curvature conventions of 7+1.nb)} & True \\
476 & \texttt{the Higgs boson on the brane (h = Omega\textasciicircum{}2 eta, Omega = 1 + r/\allowbreak{}Lambda + a2 r\textasciicircum{}2, phi\_\allowbreak{}H = v + h, gamma = v/\allowbreak{}Lambda): the terms linear in (h, r) are total derivatives, and to second order L == (1/\allowbreak{}2)(dh)\textasciicircum{}2 + (1/\allowbreak{}2)(1 + 6 xi gamma\textasciicircum{}2)(dr)\textasciicircum{}2 + 6 xi gamma dh.dr - (1/\allowbreak{}2) m\_\allowbreak{}h0\textasciicircum{}2 h\textasciicircum{}2 - (1/\allowbreak{}2) m\_\allowbreak{}r0\textasciicircum{}2 r\textasciicircum{}2 up to total derivatives (Euler-Lagrange expressions in x0 and x1); a2 drops out} & True \\
477 & \texttt{canonical kinetic terms: Transpose[S].K.S == 1 for S = \{\{1, -6 xi gamma/\allowbreak{}Z\}, \{0, 1/\allowbreak{}Z\}\} if and only if Z\textasciicircum{}2 = 1 + 6 xi gamma\textasciicircum{}2 (1 - 6 xi) (the note's Z); with the note's literal kinetic terms (no 6 xi gamma\textasciicircum{}2 for the radion) it would be Z\textasciicircum{}2 = 1 - 36 xi\textasciicircum{}2 gamma\textasciicircum{}2} & True \\
478 & \texttt{mass mixing: with (h', r') = Q(theta).(h\_\allowbreak{}m, r\_\allowbreak{}m), Q = \{\{cos, -sin\}, \{sin, cos\}\}, the off-diagonal element of Transpose[Q].M'.Q is -(1/\allowbreak{}2)(M'\_\allowbreak{}11 - M'\_\allowbreak{}22) sin 2 theta + M'\_\allowbreak{}12 cos 2 theta, zero for tan 2 theta = 2 M'\_\allowbreak{}12/\allowbreak{}(M'\_\allowbreak{}11 - M'\_\allowbreak{}22) = 12 gamma xi Z m\_\allowbreak{}h0\textasciicircum{}2/\allowbreak{}(m\_\allowbreak{}r0\textasciicircum{}2 - m\_\allowbreak{}h0\textasciicircum{}2 (Z\textasciicircum{}2 - 36 xi\textasciicircum{}2 gamma\textasciicircum{}2)) (the note's formula); with the inverse matrix (the note's display) it is +(1/\allowbreak{}2)(M'\_\allowbreak{}11 - M'\_\allowbreak{}22) sin 2 theta + M'\_\allowbreak{}12 cos 2 theta (theta -> -theta); (h, r) = S.Q.(h\_\allowbreak{}m, r\_\allowbreak{}m) gives C\_\allowbreak{}h = cos theta - (6 xi gamma/\allowbreak{}Z) sin theta, C\_\allowbreak{}r = sin theta/\allowbreak{}Z} & True \\
479 & \texttt{Higgs-radion mixing in 7+1 (gamma = v/\allowbreak{}(Sqrt[3] M\_\allowbreak{}Pl), xi = 1, m\_\allowbreak{}r0 = 10 GeV, m\_\allowbreak{}h0 = 125.20 GeV; 60-digit arithmetic): |Z - 1| < 10\textasciicircum{}-30, |theta| = 3.5 10\textasciicircum{}-16, the rates of h -> gamma gamma and h -> g g change by less than 10\textasciicircum{}-30; in RS1 with <mu> = 3 TeV gamma = v/\allowbreak{}(3 TeV) = 0.082} & True \\*
\end{longtable}}

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{5. Process and environment}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{5. Process and environment}
\begin{itemize}
\item Workspace: \texttt{D:\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Developer\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}github\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}7+1} on Windows; Python and LaTeX ran in a Linux shell with that folder mounted. Intermediate files go to \texttt{build/\allowbreak{}}, which the repository's \texttt{.gitignore} excludes.
\item The notebook is generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}make\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py} (cells written with \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}nbwrite.py}), evaluated in place by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl} in Wolfram 15.0.1, and cross-checked by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verify\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py}.
\item Python 3.13.17 with sympy 1.14.0 and numpy 2.5.3 for the documentation run of 2026-10-28 (exact rational arithmetic for every identity; the first stage was checked with Python 3.10.12, sympy 1.14.0 and numpy 2.2.6).
\item The documentation is generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}make\_\allowbreak{}docs.py} from the modules \texttt{docgen.py}, \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}common.py}, \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}dirac16\_\allowbreak{}common.py}, \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}readme.py}, \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}symbols.py} and \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}provenance.py} of the folder tools/ (and the number formatting of \texttt{doc\_\allowbreak{}dirac16.py}): one source renders Markdown and LaTeX, and every matrix, table and check list is computed or parsed, never typed. The LaTeX is compiled with XeLaTeX (TeX Live 2022, \texttt{latexmk -xelatex}, fonts Latin Modern and DejaVu Sans Mono).
\item History: the first algebraic experiments were made in a temporary cloud sandbox and then moved into \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}} of the workspace, where everything was re-run. A Wolfram 15.0 kernel window was opened once by mistake before Wolfram 15.0.1 was available; no result comes from it. Only Wolfram 15.0.1 evaluated 7+1.nb.
\item Corrections made during the review: an earlier draft called the real on-shell amplitudes of notebook section 5.4 "Majorana spinors"; that label was removed, and section 5.5 was added to show which real spinor FIELDS exist (massless with 16 components, massive only with the 32×32 matrices).
\item The evaluated notebook was rendered section by section in the Wolfram 15.0.1 front end (\texttt{Rasterize}) and inspected. Characters that the front end's text font shows badly are written differently in text cells: the double-struck O and the arrow of $\vec x$ as small inline formulas, the script $\ell$ in the font Cambria Math, and $\not p$, $\not k$ as \texttt{pslash}, \texttt{kslash}.
\item Second stage (sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb): all Wolfram Language work ran in the front end \texttt{C:\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Program Files\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Wolfram Research\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}Wolfram\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}15.0.1\textbackslash{}\allowbreak{}WolframNB.exe}. Jobs were requested without keyboard input through \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}watch\_\allowbreak{}requests.wl} (loaded once in that front end; every 10 seconds it reads \texttt{build/\allowbreak{}request.txt} and evaluates \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}git\_\allowbreak{}push.wl} or a development file); new cells were developed with \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}eval\_\allowbreak{}cells\_\allowbreak{}log.wl}, which evaluates a generated notebook expression by expression, logs messages and checks, and stops at the first failure. \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl} evaluates with a time limit of one hour. Pushes run the local Git for Windows from the front end (\texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}git\_\allowbreak{}push.wl}, no login prompt) to \texttt{https:/\allowbreak{}/\allowbreak{}github.com/\allowbreak{}once-ere/\allowbreak{}7plus1.git}, the new name of the repository.
\item Second stage, Python: \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}dirac16.py} (sympy, mpmath, numpy) is the independent reference; \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verify\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py} was extended to sections 7–9 and to the comparison of sections 0–6 with the backup of the first stage.
\item Third stage (sections 11–19 and the summary of all checks, then section 20): the new sections were first evaluated cell by cell in a fresh parallel kernel of the same front end (a development file runs the input cells of sections 0–6, 7.1, 7.10 and 11–19 and writes a progress line around every cell and the list of failed checks); this exposed a name clash (a helper named like the zero vector \texttt{zero8} of section 1, renamed \texttt{asMat8}) and a formulation of the exponential solutions that did not finish (xAct with symbolic exponentials; replaced by the substitution into the xAct components of the cosmological metric). The complete notebook was then evaluated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl} as before.
\item Third stage, Python: \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}lovelock71.py} (curvature and pair-set Lovelock engine), \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}gravity.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}ks\_\allowbreak{}gravity.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}sm.py} (sympy, numpy, scipy, mpmath) are the independent references; the documentation of the third stage is generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}stage3\_\allowbreak{}common.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}gravity*.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}ks\_\allowbreak{}gravity*.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}sm*.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}readme\_\allowbreak{}stage3.py}.
\item Fourth stage (sections 20–23, appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 24): the new sections were developed in a development notebook — the definitions of sections 1, 11, 12 and 14 that they use, followed by sections 20–23 — evaluated cell by cell with \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}eval\_\allowbreak{}cells\_\allowbreak{}log.wl} in a fresh kernel. This exposed a name clash (the unknowns of \texttt{NDSolveValue} were first named like the down-type Yukawa matrix \texttt{yD} of section 17; they, and every symbol that could clash with an abstract index of xAct, were renamed, e.g. \texttt{rIyV}, \texttt{rIyC}, \texttt{rIIyK}, \texttt{rIIIyD}) and machine-precision data passed to functions with a \texttt{WorkingPrecision} (removed). The complete notebook was then evaluated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl}.
\item Fourth stage, Python: \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}coupled.py} (numpy, scipy, sympy, mpmath) is the independent reference; the documentation of the fourth stage is generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}coupled.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}coupled\_\allowbreak{}symbols.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}coupled\_\allowbreak{}provenance.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}readme\_\allowbreak{}stage4.py}.
\item Section 24 (dirac16complex as dark matter and dark energy, appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 25): the data were taken from the papers of the collaborations (their abstracts and tables, read on 27 October 2026); the new section was evaluated with the complete notebook by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl}. A first evaluation produced one message (a line-search warning of \texttt{FindRoot} for the largest stable mass, whose closed form $(K/(c_y^2t_0))^{1/3}$ is now used); the second and later evaluations have no message. \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}dark.py} is the independent reference; the documents are generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}dark.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}dark\_\allowbreak{}symbols.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}dark\_\allowbreak{}provenance.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}readme\_\allowbreak{}stage5.py}.
\item Sections 25 and 26 (the auxiliary torus and the world brane; the radion on the 4-torus and on the 4-sphere, appended on 28 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 26 and then section 27): the new parts of the notebook were tested before each full evaluation with standalone Wolfram files that run only the new input cells (messages are reported per expression); the complete notebook was then evaluated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}run\_\allowbreak{}eval.wl} with no message. Printed numbers are computed by the notebook, never typed. \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}torus\_\allowbreak{}brane.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}radion.py} are the independent references; the documents are generated by \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}torus*.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}brane*.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}radion*.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}torus\_\allowbreak{}brane\_\allowbreak{}common.py}, \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}radion\_\allowbreak{}common.py} and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}doc\_\allowbreak{}readme\_\allowbreak{}stage6.py}.
\end{itemize}
\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{6. The second stage: sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{6. The second stage: sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb}
This section records the provenance of sections 7–10 of 7+1.nb: the Dirac equation and the Kohn–Sham theories of the field dirac16complex. Notation ($\Psi$ is the field dirac16complex):
\begin{gather*}
\Gamma_A=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A,\qquad \Gamma^{A}=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AA}\Gamma_A,\qquad \Gamma^0=\Gamma_0,\qquad \chi=\chi^{(1,7)}=i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8,\\ \bar\Psi=\Psi^\dagger\Gamma^0,\qquad \vec x=(x_1,\dots,x_7),\qquad \vec p=(p^1,\dots,p^7).
\end{gather*}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{6.1 The task}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{6.1 The task}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Back up 7+1.nb and append to it; the first part stays unchanged.
\item A checked Dirac equation on the real 7+1 spacetime for a 16-component quantized fermion field named dirac16complex whose components are complex-valued Grassmann operators, with eight PURELY REAL anticommuting 16×16 Dirac matrices taken from 7+1.nb.
\item Chiral projections writing the 16-component equation as two 8-component equations, coupled when the mass is not zero.
\item A 16-component Kohn–Sham theory of the field coupled to itself, and a chiral version using the chiral projections.
\item Complete, checked documentation (every document with a provenance file and a symbol file, Markdown and LaTeX, compiled to PDF, each self-contained); every completed stage pushed to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 (the repository was renamed from 7+1 to 7plus1); only the Wolfram front end 15.0.1 is used.
\end{enumerate}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{6.2 The first part of the notebook}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{6.2 The first part of the notebook}
The evaluated notebook of the first stage was copied, in the workspace, to the file below (the folder backups/ is excluded from the repository by its .gitignore). \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verify\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py} compares every input, text and section cell of sections 0–6 of the new 7+1.nb with that copy: every cell is present, and each is identical except the cells changed or added by the explanation of the signature of 27 October 2026 (section 1.4).

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{6.3 Derivations and decisions}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{6.3 Derivations and decisions}
\textbf{Dirac matrices.} The real generators $\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A$ of section 2.5 are used unchanged: $\Gamma_0=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&1_8\\1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, $\Gamma_i=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}\tau^{O}_i&0\\0&-\tau^{O}_i\end{smallmatrix}\right)$, $\Gamma_A\Gamma_B+\Gamma_B\Gamma_A=2\eta^{(1,7)}_{AB}1_{16}$. The opposite sign convention $\eta^{(7,1)}$ has no real 16×16 realization (section 2.7), so this is the only real choice. \textbf{The factor $i$.} With real $\Gamma_A$, $(\Gamma^A\partial_A)^2=\frac12\lbrace\Gamma^A,\Gamma^B\rbrace\partial_A\partial_B=\partial_0^2-\partial_1^2-\dots-\partial_7^2$, hence
\begin{align*}
\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A+m\big)\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)&=-\big(\partial_0^2-\partial_1^2-\dots-\partial_7^2+m^2\big)1_{16}, \\
\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)&=i\partial_0-H_{\rm D},\qquad H_{\rm D}=-i\Gamma^0\Gamma^i\partial_i+m\Gamma^0 .
\end{align*}
\textbf{Real amplitudes.} For $e^{-i(Ex_0-\vec p\cdot\vec x)}$, with $P_\tau=p^i\tau^{O}_i$ ($P_\tau^{\mathsf T}=-P_\tau$, $P_\tau^2=-\vec p^{\,2}1_8$):
\begin{align*}
H_{\rm D}(\vec p)&=\begin{pmatrix}0&m1_8+P_\tau\\m1_8-P_\tau&0\end{pmatrix},\qquad (m1_8+P_\tau)(m1_8-P_\tau)=(m^2+\vec p^{\,2})1_8=E^21_8, \\
H_{\rm D}(\vec p)\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}&=\begin{pmatrix}E^2e_s\\E(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}=E\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix}, \\
w_s&=\frac1{\sqrt E}\begin{pmatrix}E\,e_s\\(m1_8-P_\tau)e_s\end{pmatrix},\qquad w_s^{\mathsf T}w_r=2E\,\delta_{sr}.
\end{align*}
\textbf{Chirality.} The real matrices anticommuting with every $\Gamma_A$ are $c\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8$ (section 2.9), with square $-c^21_{16}$; the chirality is therefore the complex $\chi=i\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0&-i1_8\\i1_8&0\end{smallmatrix}\right)$. With $E_\pm$ the 16×8 matrices $\frac1{\sqrt2}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1_8\\-i1_8\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ and $\frac1{\sqrt2}\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-i1_8\\1_8\end{smallmatrix}\right)$ ($\chi E_-=-E_-$, $\chi E_+=E_+$, $E_-E_-^\dagger=P^{(1,7)}_L$, $E_+E_+^\dagger=P^{(1,7)}_R$), $\Psi_L=E_-^\dagger\Psi$, $\Psi_R=E_+^\dagger\Psi$ and
\begin{align*}
E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0E_+&=E_+^\dagger\Gamma^0E_-=1_8,\qquad E_\mp^\dagger\Gamma^0E_\mp=0,\qquad E_\mp^\dagger\Gamma^0\Gamma^iE_\mp=\mp i\tau^{O}_i,\qquad E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0\Gamma^iE_+=0, \\
E_-^\dagger\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi&=\big(i\partial_0+\tau^{O}_i\partial_i\big)\Psi_L-m\Psi_R,\qquad E_+^\dagger\Gamma^0\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi=\big(i\partial_0-\tau^{O}_i\partial_i\big)\Psi_R-m\Psi_L .
\end{align*}
\textbf{Self-coupling.} The four Lorentz-invariant contact interactions without derivatives used in relativistic point-coupling models (scalar, pseudoscalar, vector, axial vector) with real couplings, and real sources that define the densities:
\begin{align*}
\mathcal L&=\bar\Psi\big(i\Gamma^{A}\partial_A-m\big)\Psi-\bar\Psi\big(\mathsf s+i\chi\mathsf p+\Gamma^{A}(\mathsf v_A+\chi\mathsf a_A)\big)\Psi \\
&\quad-\frac{g_S}2(\bar\Psi\Psi)^2-\frac{g_P}2(\bar\Psi i\chi\Psi)^2-\frac{g_V}2(\bar\Psi\Gamma^{A}\Psi)(\bar\Psi\Gamma_A\Psi)-\frac{g_A}2(\bar\Psi\Gamma^{A}\chi\Psi)(\bar\Psi\Gamma_A\chi\Psi).
\end{align*}
The tensor and higher-rank channels are not included. With $\hbar=c=1$ every $g$ has mass dimension $-6$ (an effective theory: no-sea approximation, or a momentum cutoff for the sea). \textbf{Hohenberg–Kohn.} For $H[w]=H_0+H_{\rm int}+\int w\cdot n$ with $w\cdot n=\mathsf s\rho_S+\mathsf p\rho_P+\mathsf v_Aj^A+\mathsf a_Aj_5^A$, two sources $w\neq w'$ with different ground states and the same densities would give $E<E'+\int(w-w')\cdot n$ and $E'<E+\int(w'-w)\cdot n$, so $E+E'<E+E'$; hence $E[w]=\min_n(F[n]+\int w\cdot n)$. \textbf{Kohn–Sham.} Varying $T_s+\int w\cdot n+E_H+E_{xc}$ with respect to $\Phi_k^\dagger$ under $\int\Phi_k^\dagger\Phi_l=\delta_{kl}$ (multipliers $\lambda_k$), with $\delta\rho_S/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Phi$, $\delta\rho_P/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0i\chi\Phi$, $\delta j^A/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Gamma^A\Phi$, $\delta j_5^A/\delta\Phi^\dagger=\Gamma^0\Gamma^A\chi\Phi$, gives $H_{\rm KS}\Phi_k=\lambda_k\Phi_k$ with
\begin{align*}
H_{\rm KS}&=\Gamma^0\Gamma^i(-i\partial_i+\mathcal V_i)+\Gamma^0(m+\mathcal S)+\mathcal V_0+\Gamma^0i\chi\mathcal P+\chi\mathcal A_0+\Gamma^0\Gamma^i\chi\mathcal A_i, \\
\mathcal S&=\mathsf s+g_S\rho_S+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta\rho_S},\qquad \mathcal P=\mathsf p+g_P\rho_P+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta\rho_P}, \\
\mathcal V_A&=\mathsf v_A+g_Vj_A+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta j^A},\qquad \mathcal A_A=\mathsf a_A+g_Aj_{5A}+\frac{\delta E_{xc}}{\delta j_5^A},
\end{align*}
where $E_H=\int[\frac{g_S}2\rho_S^2+\frac{g_P}2\rho_P^2+\frac{g_V}2j^Aj_A+\frac{g_A}2j_5^Aj_{5A}]$ and $T_s=\sum_k\int\Phi_k^\dagger H_{\rm D}\Phi_k$. \textbf{Decision (no sea):} the orbitals are the occupied positive-energy states. \textbf{Exchange.} Wick’s theorem for a Slater determinant, $\langle c_a^\dagger c_c^\dagger c_dc_b\rangle=\varrho_{ba}\varrho_{dc}-\varrho_{da}\varrho_{bc}$ with $\varrho_{xy}=\sum_k\Phi_{k,x}\Phi_{k,y}^*$ and $\mathcal R=\varrho\,\Gamma^0$, gives $\langle:(\bar\Psi O_1\Psi)(\bar\Psi O_2\Psi):\rangle=\mathrm{tr}(O_1\mathcal R)\mathrm{tr}(O_2\mathcal R)-\mathrm{tr}(O_1\mathcal RO_2\mathcal R)$, hence the exact exchange energy
\begin{equation*}
E_x=-\frac{g_S}2\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal R\mathcal R)-\frac{g_P}2\mathrm{tr}(i\chi\mathcal R\,i\chi\mathcal R)-\frac{g_V}2\mathrm{tr}(\Gamma^{A}\mathcal R\Gamma_A\mathcal R)-\frac{g_A}2\mathrm{tr}(\Gamma^{A}\chi\mathcal R\Gamma_A\chi\mathcal R).
\end{equation*}
\textbf{Chiral Kohn–Sham.} With $\Phi_{kL}=E_-^\dagger\Phi_k$, $\Phi_{kR}=E_+^\dagger\Phi_k$ and the blocks above, $\kappa=\sum_k\Phi_{kL}^\dagger\Phi_{kR}$, $\rho_S=\kappa+\kappa^*$, $\rho_P=i(\kappa-\kappa^*)$, $\mathcal M=m+\mathcal S+i\mathcal P$, $\mathcal V^{L,R}_A=\mathcal V_A\mp\mathcal A_A$:
\begin{align*}
\big[\mathcal V^L_0-\tau^{O}_i\partial_i-i\tau^{O}_i\mathcal V^L_i\big]\Phi_L+\mathcal M\Phi_R&=\lambda\Phi_L, \\
\mathcal M^*\Phi_L+\big[\mathcal V^R_0+\tau^{O}_i\partial_i+i\tau^{O}_i\mathcal V^R_i\big]\Phi_R&=\lambda\Phi_R, \\
e^{i\vartheta\chi}&=\cos\vartheta\,1_{16}-\sin\vartheta\,\Gamma^{(1,7)}_8:\qquad \kappa\to e^{2i\vartheta}\kappa, \\
\Delta E_H&=\frac{g_S-g_P}2\big[(e^{4i\vartheta}-1)\kappa^2+(e^{-4i\vartheta}-1)\kappa^{*2}\big].
\end{align*}
\textbf{Uniform matter.} Units $m=1$; all 8 spin states filled to $k_F$; $|S^6|=16\pi^3/15$ is the area of the unit sphere in $\mathbb R^7$ and $c_7=8|S^6|/(2\pi)^7=1/(15\pi^4)$. With the energy density $\mathcal E$, its kinetic part $\mathcal E_{\rm kin}$, $\rho_S$ and the density $\rho$:
\begin{align*}
\rho&=\frac{k_F^7}{105\pi^4},\qquad \rho_S=c_7\int_0^{k_F}\frac{k^6m^*\,dk}{\sqrt{k^2+m^{*2}}},\qquad \mathcal E_{\rm kin}=c_7\int_0^{k_F}k^6\sqrt{k^2+m^{*2}}\,dk, \\
m^*&=m+g_S\rho_S(m^*),\qquad \mathcal E=\mathcal E_{\rm kin}-\frac{(m^*-m)^2}{2g_S}+\frac{g_V}2\rho^2, \\
E/A&=\frac{\mathcal E}{\rho},\qquad E_F=g_V\rho+\sqrt{k_F^2+m^{*2}} .
\end{align*}
Because $\partial\mathcal E/\partial m^*=0$ is the gap equation, $d\mathcal E/d\rho=E_F$ at every density, and $\frac{d}{dk_F}\frac{\mathcal E}\rho=\frac{c_7k_F^6}{\rho}(E_F-\frac{\mathcal E}{\rho})$ vanishes at saturation: $E/A=E_F$ (Hugenholtz–Van Hove). \textbf{Decisions:} $g_S=-10^5$, $g_V=10^4$, $g_P=g_A=0$ (binding and saturation); root finding by Brent’s method with 50-digit (gap equation) and 40-digit (saturation) working precision. \textbf{Dirac sea.} With a cutoff $\Lambda_{\rm c}$, $g_S=g_P=g$ and a uniform $\mathcal M$:
\begin{align*}
J(M)&=\frac1{120\pi^4}\int_0^{\Lambda_{\rm c}}\frac{q^6dq}{\sqrt{q^2+M^2}},\qquad K(M)=\frac1{120\pi^4}\int_0^{\Lambda_{\rm c}}q^6\sqrt{q^2+M^2}\,dq, \\
\kappa&=-4\mathcal M^*J(|\mathcal M|),\qquad \mathcal M=m-8gJ(|\mathcal M|)\mathcal M,\qquad g_c=-\frac1{8J(0)}=-\frac{90\pi^4}{\Lambda_{\rm c}^6}, \\
\mathcal E_{\rm vac}&=-8K+8J\,\mathrm{Re}[(\mathcal M-m)\mathcal M^*]+32g|\mathcal M|^2J^2 .
\end{align*}
\textbf{Decisions:} $\Lambda_{\rm c}=1$, $g=\frac65g_c$, and $m=\frac1{100}$ for the explicit-breaking example.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{6.4 Literature used in the second stage}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{6.4 Literature used in the second stage}
\begin{itemize}
\item P. A. M. Dirac, Proc. R. Soc. Lond. A 117 (1928) 610 — $(i\gamma^\mu\partial_\mu-m)\psi=0$, $\gamma^\mu\gamma^\nu+\gamma^\nu\gamma^\mu=2\eta^{\mu\nu}$.
\item P. Hohenberg and W. Kohn, Phys. Rev. 136 (1964) B864 — $E[v]=\min_n(F[n]+\int vn)$; W. Kohn and L. J. Sham, Phys. Rev. 140 (1965) A1133 — $[-\frac12\nabla^2+v+v_H+v_{xc}]\varphi_k=\varepsilon_k\varphi_k$, $n=\sum_k|\varphi_k|^2$ (atomic units).
\item A. K. Rajagopal and J. Callaway, Phys. Rev. B 7 (1973) 1912; R. M. Dreizler and E. K. U. Gross, \emph{Density Functional Theory}, Springer (1990) — the energy as a functional of the current $j^A$, the Dirac form of the Kohn–Sham equations, the no-sea approximation.
\item G. C. Wick, Phys. Rev. 80 (1950) 268; M. Fierz, Z. Phys. 104 (1937) 553 — contractions in a Slater determinant (above), and $\sum_K(\Gamma_K)_{ab}(\Gamma_K^{-1})_{cd}=16\delta_{ad}\delta_{cb}$ for the 256 products $\Gamma_K$ of the generators.
\item J. D. Walecka, Ann. Phys. 83 (1974) 491 — mean-field saturation of uniform matter, $m^*=m-\frac{g_\sigma^2}{m_\sigma^2}\rho_S$, $V_0=\frac{g_\omega^2}{m_\omega^2}\rho$ (here $g_S=-g_\sigma^2/m_\sigma^2$, $g_V=g_\omega^2/m_\omega^2$); N. M. Hugenholtz and L. Van Hove, Physica 24 (1958) 363 — $E/A=E_F$ at saturation.
\item Y. Nambu and G. Jona-Lasinio, Phys. Rev. 122 (1961) 345 — $M=m-2G\langle\bar\psi\psi\rangle$ with a cutoff and a critical coupling (here $G=-g/2$).
\end{itemize}

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{7. The third stage: sections 11–19 of 7+1.nb}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{7. The third stage: sections 11–19 of 7+1.nb}
This section records the provenance of the gravity, Kohn–Sham and Standard Model sections (11–19) and of the final summary (then section 20, now section 27). Notation: $\Gamma_A=\Gamma^{(1,7)}_A$, $\Gamma^A=\eta_{(1,7)}^{AA}\Gamma_A$, $\eta=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$ (simply the squares of the octonion basis, $\eta_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)$), $\Psi$ the field dirac16complex.

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{7.1 The task}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{7.1 The task}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Rebuild the ideas of github.com/once-ere/Dirac\_claude on the 7+1 spacetime of this project (one time coordinate, seven space coordinates; never the 4+4 metric of Dirac\_claude): the Einstein and Lovelock tensors; generalized frame matrices; a canonical spin connection, with a vanishing total covariant derivative, that couples gravity to dirac16complex; the Kohn–Sham theory of dirac16complex in gravitational fields in the primordial (near $x_0=0$), modern and far-future epochs; a generalized self-coupling; and the totality of the Standard Model.
\item Compute the Lovelock tensors as in the author’s corrected reference notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb, and do not use its section "FAILED Model metric g71; DO NOT USE!".
\item Document every stage (documentation, provenance and symbol files; Markdown, LaTeX, PDF; self-contained) and push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1.
\item Make clear in all documentation, examples and provenance records that the xAct declaration $\{7,1,0\}$ declares seven space-like dimensions and one time-like dimension, while $\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis (the author’s instruction of 27 October 2026).
\end{enumerate}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{7.2 Inputs and how they were used}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{7.2 Inputs and how they were used}
The reference notebook Einstein-Lovelock-7+1-Nash.nb (P. L. Nash, 2022, GPL; the author’s corrected version) supplied: the xAct packages xCoba and xTras; the manifold with abstract indices $a$, $b$, $c$, $d$, $f$, $i$, $j$, $k$, $l$, $m$, $n$, $p$, $q$, $r$, $s$, $y$; the chart with coordinate scalars \texttt{x0[]}, …, \texttt{x7[]}; \texttt{MyArrayComponents}; the generalized delta \texttt{kδ}; the three Lovelock tensors with their normalization
\begin{equation*}
\mathrm{Lovelock}_k=-\frac1{2^{k+1}}\,R_{c_1d_1}{}^{f_1i_1}\cdots R_{c_kd_k}{}^{f_ki_k}\,\delta^{b\,c_1d_1\cdots c_kd_k}_{a\,f_1i_1\cdots f_ki_k}\quad(k=1,2,3);
\end{equation*}
the evaluation of components by \texttt{CD = LC[g]}; and the vacuum field equations $0=-\Lambda\delta+H^{-2}w_1\mathrm{Lovelock}_1+H^{-4}w_2\mathrm{Lovelock}_2+H^{-6}w_3\mathrm{Lovelock}_3$ with the procedure of solving them for $w_2,w_3$. The model metric of its FAILED section was not used anywhere. Taken unchanged as well: the signature declaration \texttt{DefMetric[\{7, 1, 0\}, g71[-a, -b], CD, …]} — in xAct the list $\{7,1,0\}$ declares seven space-like dimensions, one time-like dimension and no degenerate dimension, exactly the 7+1 spacetime of this project. It is not to be confused with the matrix $\eta=\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$, which is simply the squares of the octonion basis ($e_0^2=+1$, $e_i^2=-1$, $\eta_{AB}=\mathrm{Re}(e_Ae_B)$) and gives the signs of the metric components ($g_{00}>0$). \textbf{A correction (27 October 2026):} an earlier version of 7+1.nb declared $\{1,7,0\}$, reading the list as the signs of $\eta$; the author pointed out that $\{7,1,0\}$ declares seven space-like and one time-like dimension while $\mathrm{diag}(+1,-1,\dots,-1)$ is simply the squares of the octonion basis, and 7+1.nb now declares $\{7,1,0\}$ as the reference notebook does. No Lovelock component changed: they follow from the component matrix of the metric, and \texttt{SignDetOfMetric} is $-1$ for both lists (checked with xTensor 1.2.0). xAct versions as printed by the notebook: xPerm 1.2.3, xTensor 1.2.0, xCoba 0.8.6, xTras 1.4.2.

2 & the Lovelock tensors are computed twice (xAct; pair-set sums) & exact agreement is an independent check of the reference method \\

4 & the canonical (Levi-Civita) spin connection, $\Omega_\mu=\frac14\omega_{\mu AB}\Gamma^A\Gamma^B$ & it is the connection with $\nabla_\mu\Gamma_\nu=0$ \\
5 & the self-coupling is organized by the nine ranks of the Clifford algebra & each rank is one Lorentz-invariant class; the Fierz matrix closes on them \\
6 & Kohn–Sham matter: homogeneous, spin-saturated, no Dirac sea, adiabatic & the cosmological spacetime is homogeneous; the expansion rates are small compared with the orbital energies \\
7 & the Standard Model on a 3+1 brane of a torus of the auxiliary coordinates & the 7+1 analogue of the fold of Dirac\_claude \\
8 & portals are invented local couplings at the brane & the only contact of dirac16complex with the Standard Model besides gravity \\*

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{8. The fourth stage: sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{8. The fourth stage: sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb}
This section records the provenance of the coupled field equations and their solutions in the three gravity regimes (sections 20–23 of 7+1.nb, appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 24).

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{8.1 The task}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{8.1 The task}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Compute accurate approximate solutions of the coupled field equations of the model for all three gravity regimes; write each and every interaction in equation form; define all symbols; explain and analyze the solutions (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
\item Document them in a documentation file with a provenance file and a symbol file, in Markdown and LaTeX, the LaTeX compiled to PDF; no document refers the reader to another file, and every equation used is written in the file that uses it.
\item Then analyze whether dirac16complex can be the dark matter and the dark energy, on the basis of these solutions (a separate stage).
\item Push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 and verify the repository.
\end{enumerate}
\textbf{Interpretation.} The "three gravity regimes" are the three epochs of the model: primordial (near $x_0=0$; inside it the cubic, Gauss–Bonnet and Einstein terms dominate in turn), modern (a stabilized auxiliary space, four-dimensional gravity on the brane) and far future (de Sitter).

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{8.2 Inputs, data and decisions}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{8.2 Inputs, data and decisions}
Inputs from the earlier sections: the closed forms of the Lovelock tensors of the cosmological metric (section 12.6), the pair-set densities (section 12.5), the Kasner forms (section 14.1). Data: Particle Data Group 2024/2025 (masses), Planck 2018 ($H_0=67.4$ km/s/Mpc, $\Omega_m=0.315$), $T_{\rm CMB}=2.7255$ K, $N_{\rm eff}=3.044$, $M_{\rm Pl}=2.435\times10^{18}$ GeV. Choices: $M_8=10$ TeV, $\alpha_2=1/M_8^2$, $\alpha_3=10^{-6}/M_8^4$, $m_r=10$ GeV, $f_W=M_8^4$; in regime I units $\ell_2=1$, $\xi=10^{-6}$, and a massless collisionless dirac16complex gas normalized to $\kappa\varepsilon=10^{-6}\times3H_a^2$ at $a=b=1$, $H_a=1$; the size of a curvature term is the sum of the absolute values of its monomials (in a Kasner regime the net value cancels). Before the new sections were appended the evaluated notebook was copied to \texttt{backups/\allowbreak{}7+1\_\allowbreak{}2026-10-27\_\allowbreak{}before\_\allowbreak{}coupled.nb} (excluded by .gitignore).

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{9. Section 24 of 7+1.nb: dirac16complex as dark matter and dark energy}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{9. Section 24 of 7+1.nb: dirac16complex as dark matter and dark energy}
This section records the provenance of section 24 of 7+1.nb (appended on 27 October 2026; the summary of all checks became section 25, and is now section 27).

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{9.1 The task}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{9.1 The task}
\begin{enumerate}
\item On the basis of the accurate approximate solutions of the coupled field equations in the three gravity regimes, analyze the validity of associating dark matter (and dark energy) with dirac16complex (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
\item Document the analysis in a documentation file with a provenance file and a symbol file, in Markdown and LaTeX, the LaTeX compiled to PDF; no document refers the reader to another file, and every equation used is written in the file that uses it.
\item Push every completed stage to github.com/once-ere/7plus1 and verify the repository.
\end{enumerate}
\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{9.2 Inputs, data and decisions}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{9.2 Inputs, data and decisions}
Inputs from the earlier sections: the stress tensor from the comoving energy (section 20.2), the radion equation (20.3), the gas-dominated regime without stabilizer (21.3), the gas share after capture (22.1), the radion decay rates into brane fields and into the bulk with Jacobi’s $r_4(k)$ (22.2), the late universe (22.4), the Standard Model constants (17.3), the uniform Kohn–Sham matter (8.7). Data: Planck 2018; DESI DR2 (arXiv:2503.14738) and Garcia-Quintero et al. (arXiv:2504.18464); LZ (arXiv:2410.17036); Randall et al. (Bullet Cluster); Iršič et al. (Lyman-$\alpha$); Ballesteros, Garcia and Pierre (decay-produced dark matter); Alvey et al. (Pauli bound, $g=2$, scaling $g^{-1/4}$); Roach et al. (NuSTAR); Particle Data Group 2024 (large extra dimensions). Decisions: natural couplings at $M_8$; Majorana normalization of the sterile-neutrino decay rates; instantaneous reheating at $T_{\rm RH}\le T_*$; production by Higgs decays as a lower bound; continuum Kaluza–Klein sums checked against lattice sums; Pauli bound for $g=16$. Before section 24 was appended the evaluated notebook was copied to \texttt{backups/\allowbreak{}7+1\_\allowbreak{}2026-10-27\_\allowbreak{}before\_\allowbreak{}dark.nb} (excluded by .gitignore). One correction to section 23.3 was made: the cold-dark-matter density in its table read "0.265" and now reads "Omega\_c = 0.264" ($0.120/0.674^2=0.26416$).

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{10. Sections 25–26 of 7+1.nb: the auxiliary torus, the world brane and the radion}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{10. Sections 25–26 of 7+1.nb: the auxiliary torus, the world brane and the radion}

\Needspace{8\baselineskip}\subsection*{10.1 The task}\addcontentsline{toc}{subsection}{10.1 The task}
\begin{enumerate}
\item Define and explain the "auxiliary torus" and the "world brane" precisely and correctly, each in its own documentation, symbol and provenance files (Markdown, LaTeX, PDF), for students, with examples (summarized; the original wording is the author’s private working material).
\item Read a note of the author on the radion and use its ideas to verify and clarify the radion and brane documents; create examples in which the four extra space dimensions are a 4-sphere and a 4-torus.

The independent programs \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}torus\_\allowbreak{}brane.py} (37 boolean checks, 37 True) and \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}ref\_\allowbreak{}radion.py} (31, 31 True) recompute every number with their own code; the 17 and 15 groups of numbers printed by the notebook and by them agree to a largest relative difference of $3.8\times10^{-10}$ and $8.2\times10^{-11}$; their complete outputs are printed in section 4.3. \texttt{tools/\allowbreak{}verify\_\allowbreak{}7p1\_\allowbreak{}nb.py} confirms that, with each new section removed, the notebook equals its backup.

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}\section*{11. Authorship}\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{11. Authorship}
